\varphi(x)=\tfrac{x}{1-\exp(-x)},\quad x>0.

Clearly,
\varphi(x)\rightarrow 1
as
x\rightarrow 0
.
Take now
\lambda=6C
and
p\varepsilon^{2}
large enough so that

\varphi(\tfrac{\lambda T}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}})=\varphi(\tfrac{6CT}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}})
is less than
2
.
We have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{\ell T}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\underset{\small\omega\in\Omega}{\textrm{\rm essup}}\bigl|h_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}-\tfrac{1}{\varpi}h_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\bigr|^{2}\\
&\leq\tfrac{1}{3}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{\ell T}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\underset{\small\omega\in\Omega}{\textrm{\rm essup}}\bigl|h_{\ell T/p}^{n}-\tfrac{1}{\varpi}h_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\bigr|^{2}+\tfrac{C}{1-\exp(-6CT/(p\delta\varepsilon^{2}))}\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{T}{\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\varpi^{-2n}.\end{split}

Here, we notice that (assuming without any loss of generality that
C\geq 1
)

\begin{split}&\tfrac{C}{1-\exp(-6CT/(p\delta\varepsilon^{2}))}=\tfrac{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}{6T}\varphi\Bigl(\tfrac{6CT}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigr)\leq\tfrac{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}{3T}\end{split}

and then,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{\ell T}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\underset{\small\omega\in\Omega}{\textrm{\rm essup}}\bigl|h_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}-h_{\ell T/p}^{(p),\varpi}\bigr|^{2}\\
&\leq\tfrac{1}{3}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{\ell T}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\underset{\small\omega\in\Omega}{\textrm{\rm essup}}\bigl|h_{\ell T/p}^{n}-h_{\ell T/p}^{(p),\varpi}\bigr|^{2}+C^{\prime}p\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{T}{\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\varpi^{-2n},\end{split}

for a constant
C^{\prime}
depending on the same parameters as
C
.
The above inequality is very similar to
(
2.26
). Similar to
(
2.27
), we obtain

\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{\ell T}{p\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\underset{\small\omega\in\Omega}{\textrm{\rm essup}}\bigl|h_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}-\tfrac{1}{\varpi}h_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\bigr|^{2}\leq C^{\prime}p\exp\bigl(7C\tfrac{T}{\delta\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)\varpi^{-2n},

for a possibly new value of
C^{\prime}
.
Back to
(
2.63
), we obtain
(
2.47
).
Inequality
(
2.48
)
is proven as (
2.13
).
∎

2.2.4.
Convergence to the MFG

We now study the distance between
({\bm{m}}^{(p)},{\bm{h}}^{(p)})
and
({\bm{m}},{\bm{h}})

(see (
2.11
))
as
p
tends to
\infty
.
While this looks a natural question,
the following result, which is given for reader’s interest only, has a secondary role in our study.
The limit
p\rightarrow\infty
will be addressed
in the next subsection but from another point of view.

Proposition 2.23
.

With
({\bm{m}}^{(p)},\widetilde{\bm{h}}^{(p)})

as in the statement of Theorem

2.17

(with the same extension as in Remark

2.19
)
and with

({\bm{m}},{\bm{h}})

as in the statement of
Theorem

2.4
and
(
2.14
),
there exists
a constant
C
, only depending on

d
,
T
,

\|f\|_{1,\infty}
,
\|g\|_{1,\infty}
,
|Q|
and
|R|
,
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\exp\Bigl(\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigr)\tfrac{\ln(p)}{p}.

Proof.

For an integer
p\geq 1
,
we write

\mathrm{d}m_{t}^{(p)}=-\eta^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{p},\quad t\in[0,T].

We notice that

(2.64)

\displaystyle\bigl|m_{t}^{(p)}-m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\tfrac{T}{p}|m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\sup_{0\leq s-\tau_{p}(t)\leq T/p}|W_{s}-W_{\tau_{p}(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq C\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|,\quad t\in[0,T].

Moreover,

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\bigl(m_{t}^{(p)}-m_{t}\bigr)&=-\bigl(\eta^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}-\eta_{t}m_{t}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}(W_{t}^{p}-W_{t})\\
&=-\bigl(\eta^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}-\eta^{(p)}_{t}m^{(p)}_{t}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t-\bigl(\eta_{t}^{(p)}-\eta_{t}\bigr)m_{t}^{(p)}\mathrm{d}t-\eta_{t}\bigl(m_{t}^{(p)}-m_{t}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\mathrm{d}(W_{t}^{p}-W_{t}).\end{split}

By combining the last two displays with (
2.40
), we deduce from Gronwall’s lemma that

(2.65)

\begin{split}|m_{t}^{(p)}-m_{t}|&\leq C\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|W_{s}-W_{s}^{p}|\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|\\
&\leq C\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+C\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|,\end{split}

for

t\in[0,T]
.
By Lemma

2.8

(the proof of which also provides a bound for the time-derivative of
\theta_{\varepsilon}
)
and
(
2.17
)–(
2.18
) and by the analogue of
(
2.64
) but for
{\bm{m}}
, we also have

(2.66)

\begin{split}|h_{\tau_{p}(t)}-h_{t}|&=\bigl|\theta_{\varepsilon}\bigl(\tau_{p}(t),m_{\tau_{p}(t)}\bigr)-\theta_{\varepsilon}\bigl(t,m_{t}\bigr)\bigr|\\
&\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigl(\tfrac{T}{p}+|m_{\tau_{p}(t)}-m_{t}|\Bigr)\\
&\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigl(\tfrac{T}{p}+\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}|+\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|\Bigr)\\
&\leq\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigl(\tfrac{T}{p}+\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|\Bigr).\end{split}

Next, we rewrite
the first term in
the right-hand side in
(
2.46
)
as
(which follows from
the last paragraph in Remark

2.16
)

{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{p,{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr]={\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}^{(p)}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr].

Meanwhile,

\begin{split}&\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr]-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{t}\bigr)\Bigr|\\
&\leq\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr]-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\bigr)\Bigr|+\Bigl|Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\bigr)-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{t}\bigr)\Bigr|.\end{split}

By the first line in
(
2.64
),

\begin{split}&\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr]-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\bigr)\Bigr|\leq C\Bigl(\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\sqrt{\tfrac{T}{p}}\Bigr).\end{split}

Together with (
2.65
), this gives

\begin{split}&\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr]-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{t}\bigr)\Bigr|\\
&\leq C\biggl(\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|+\varepsilon\sqrt{\tfrac{T}{p}}\biggr).\end{split}

By the above display and by
(
2.66
), we can rewrite the equation for
{\bm{h}}
in
(
2.16
) in the form:

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}h_{t}&=\Bigl\{-{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{\bm{W}}\Bigr]{\mathbf{1}}_{\{t\leq(p-1)T/p\}}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt+\Bigl(\eta_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}Q{\mathbf{1}}_{\{t\leq(p-1)T/p\}}\Bigr)h_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t+e_{t}\mathrm{d}t+k_{t}h_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon k_{t}\mathrm{d}W_{t},\end{split}

for
t\in[0,T]
, where

(2.67)

\displaystyle|e_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq C{\mathbf{1}}_{\{(p-1)T/p<t\leq T\}}+C\varepsilon\sqrt{\tfrac{T}{p}}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.0pt+C\Bigl(\tfrac{T}{p}+\tfrac{T}{p}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|m_{s}^{(p)}|+\varepsilon\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|\Bigr).

Next,
by
(
2.46
),

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\bigl[h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}\bigr]&=\Bigl(\eta_{\tau_{p(t)}+T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}Q{\mathbf{1}}_{\{t\leq(p-1)T/p\}}\Bigr)\bigl(h_{\tau_{p}(t)}-\widetilde{h}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+e_{t}\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\bigl(k_{t}h_{t}-k_{t}^{(p)}\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon\bigl(k_{t}-k_{t}^{(p)}\bigr)\mathrm{d}W_{t},\quad t\in[0,T].\end{split}

Then,
proceeding as in
(
2.22
)
and
(
2.23
) (using Lemma

2.8
),

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}&\geq-C|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-C|h_{\tau_{p}(t)}-\widetilde{h}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-2|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}||e_{t}|\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-C|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|\,|k_{t}-k_{t}^{(p)}|-C|k_{t}||h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon^{2}|k_{t}-k_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+2\varepsilon\bigl(h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}\bigr)\cdot\bigl[\bigl(k_{t}-k_{t}^{(p)}\bigr)\mathrm{d}W_{t}\bigr]\\
&\geq-\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\varepsilon^{2}|e_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-C|h_{\tau_{p}(t)}-h_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+2\varepsilon\bigl(h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}\bigr)\cdot\bigl[\bigl(k_{t}-k_{t}^{(p)}\bigr)\mathrm{d}W_{t}\bigr].\end{split}

There is one small subtlety here to handle the last term on the penultimate line because it is indexed by
time
\tau_{p}(t)
(and not
t
). The strategy is to take expectation on both sides in the above inequality and then to integrate in time
between
\tau_{p}(t)
and
t
for a given
t\in[0,T)
.
As for the dynamics of the expectation, we
have

(2.68)

\mathrm{d}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\geq-\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}t-{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|e_{t}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}t-C{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{\tau_{p}(t)}-h_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}t.

By
(
2.67
), notice that

(2.69)

{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|e_{t}|^{2}\bigr]\leq C{\mathbf{1}}_{\{(p-1)T/p<t\leq T\}}+\tfrac{C}{p^{2}}+C{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|^{2}\Bigr].

We admit for a while that

(2.70)

{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C\tfrac{\ln(p)}{p}.

And then, by
integrating
(
2.68
)
between
\tau_{p}(t)
and
t

and by invoking
boundedness of the
two processes
{\bm{h}}
and
\widetilde{\bm{h}}^{(p)}

(see Lemmas

2.10

and

2.22
), we deduce that

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{\tau_{p}(t)}-\widetilde{h}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]+\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s+\tfrac{C\ln(p)}{p}\\
&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]+\tfrac{C\ln(p)}{\varepsilon^{2}p}.\end{split}

Inserting the above estimate into
(
2.68
) and then invoking
(
2.69
), we obtain

\mathrm{d}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\geq-\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}t-C\Bigl({\mathbf{1}}_{\{(p-1)T/p<t\leq T\}}+\tfrac{\ln(p)}{\varepsilon^{2}p}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t.

By Gronwall’s lemma
(and by
(
2.65
),
which allows us to control the terminal condition), we get

{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|h_{t}-\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}|^{2}\bigr]\leq\exp\Bigl(\tfrac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigr)\tfrac{C\ln(p)}{\varepsilon^{2}p},\quad t\in[0,T].

Changing the value of
C
, we easily complete the proof of the statement.

It remains to check
(
2.70
). We have, for any
r>0
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|^{2}\geq r\Bigr\}\Bigr)\\
&=1-{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|^{2}<r\Bigr\}\Bigr)\\
&=1-{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\sup_{0\leq s\leq T/p}|W_{s}|^{2}<r\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{p}\\
&\leq 1-\bigl(1-\exp(-cpr)\bigr)^{p}\leq p\exp(-cpr),\end{split}

for a constant
c
only depending on
T
. Replacing
r
by
\ln(p)r/p
, we easily deduce that

{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\max_{k=0,\cdots,p-1}\sup_{kT/p\leq s\leq(k+1)T/p}|W_{s}-W_{kT/p}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C\tfrac{\ln(p)}{p},

which is
(
2.70
).
∎

2.2.5.
Proof of auxiliary results

Proof of Lemma
2.13
.

First Step.
We first notice that, for a given choice of
(\overline{\bm{m}}^{n},{\bm{h}}^{n})
, the problem (
2.32
) has at least one minimizer. Indeed,
the cost

{\bm{\alpha}}\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,\varpi X_{0}}(\varpi{\bm{\alpha}}+\varpi\varepsilon\dot{\bm{W}}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};\overline{\bm{m}}^{n};0)]
is lower semicontinuous with respect to
{\bm{\alpha}}

when the latter is identified with
a collection of random variables
(\alpha_{0},\cdots,\alpha_{(p-1)T/p})\in\times_{\ell=0}^{p-1}L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, with the product space being equipped with the weak topology. The identification is made possible by the fact that the controls
{\bm{\alpha}}
are chosen to be piecewise constant.
Moreover, the cost functional blows up when
the
L^{2}
-norm of

{\bm{\alpha}}
tends to
\infty
. The minimization problem can hence be restricted to a bounded subset of

\times_{\ell=0}^{p-1}L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, the latter being obviously compact for the weak topology. Existence of a minimizer easily follows.

We check below that any critical point of the cost functional is a solution of the forward-backward system
(
2.37
)–(
2.38
). Since the latter is uniquely solvable,
this indeed provides a characterization of the minimizer.

Second Step.

In order to prove that critical points solve (
2.37
)–(
2.38
),
we follow the usual lines of the Pontryagin principle.
Although the result is certainly not new, we feel better to provide the complete proof, since the formulation we use is tailor-made to our needs. We start to notice that, under the probability

{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
,
the path driven by
the control

\varpi{\bm{\alpha}}

and the initial condition

\varpi X_{0}
reads

(\varpi X_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
with

\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\alpha_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}\sigma\mathrm{d}B_{t}+\varepsilon\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T].

We then introduce an additive perturbation of the cost and hence replace
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
by

{\bm{\alpha}}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}=(\alpha_{t}+\delta\beta_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
in the above expansion, with

{\bm{\alpha}}
and
{\bm{\beta}}
being identified as before as elements of

\times_{\ell=0}^{p-1}L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. We then write
{\bm{X}}^{\delta}=(X_{t}^{\delta})_{0\leq t\leq T}

in order to emphasize the dependence of
{\bm{X}}
upon
\delta
.
When
\delta=0
, we merely write
{\bm{X}}
instead of
{\bm{X}}^{0}
.
The formal derivative with respect to
\delta
at
\delta=0
reads

\mathrm{d}\bigl(\partial X_{t}\bigr)=\beta_{t}\mathrm{d}t,\quad t\in[0,T]\,;\quad\partial X_{0}=0.

In turn,
the derivative of the cost

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,\varpi X_{0}}(\varpi[{\bm{\alpha}}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}]+\varpi\varepsilon\dot{\bm{W}}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};\overline{\bm{m}}^{n};0)]
,
with respect to
\delta
,
is

\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\delta}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,\varpi X_{0}}\Bigl(\varpi{\bm{\alpha}}+\varpi\varepsilon\dot{\bm{W}}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};\overline{\bm{m}}^{n};0\Bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr\}_{|\delta=0}\\
&=\varpi^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{n}}\biggl[\Bigl(RX_{T}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}g\bigl(\overline{m}^{n}_{T}\bigr)\Bigr)\cdot R\partial X_{T}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\{\Bigl(QX_{\tau_{p}(t)}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}f\bigl(\overline{m}^{n}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\bigr)\Bigr)\cdot Q\partial X_{\tau_{t}(p)}+\alpha_{\tau_{p}(t)}\cdot\beta_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t\biggr].\end{split}

We now solve the BSDE (under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}Y_{t}=-\bigl(Q^{\dagger}QX_{\tau_{p}(t)}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}^{n}_{\tau_{p}(t)})\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+Z_{t}^{\bm{B}}\mathrm{d}B_{t}+Z_{t}^{\bm{W}}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&Y_{T}=R^{\dagger}RX_{T}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}R^{\dagger}g\bigl(\overline{m}^{n}_{T}\bigr).\end{split}

By discrete integration by parts,
we have

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigl[Y_{T}\cdot\partial X_{T}\bigr]\\
&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\Bigl(Y_{(\ell+1)T/p}-Y_{\ell T/p}\Bigr)\cdot\partial X_{\ell T/p}+Y_{(\ell+1)T/p}\cdot\Bigl(\partial X_{(\ell+1)T/p}-\partial X_{\ell T/p}\Bigr)\Bigr]\\
&=\tfrac{T}{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[-\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}QX_{\ell T/p}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}^{n}_{\ell T/p})\Bigr)\cdot\partial X_{\ell T/p}+Y_{(\ell+1)T/p}\cdot\beta_{\ell T/p}\Bigr].\end{split}

Rewriting the above sum as an integral, we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\delta}\Bigl\{{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,\varpi X_{0}}\Bigl(\varpi{\bm{\alpha}}+\varpi\varepsilon\dot{\bm{W}}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon};\overline{\bm{m}}^{n};0\Bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr\}_{|\delta=0}\\
&=\varpi^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(Y_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}+\alpha_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr)\cdot\beta_{\tau_{p}(t)}\mathrm{d}t\biggr],\end{split}

which means that the optimizer
(which we merely write

{\bm{\alpha}}^{n+1}
without specifying the indices
p
and
\varpi
)
satisfies

(2.71)

\alpha_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}=-{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[Y_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]=-Y_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}+\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}QX_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}^{n}_{\ell T/p})\Bigr),

for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
,
where

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{n+1}=\alpha_{t}^{n+1}\mathrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}\sigma\mathrm{d}B_{t}+\varepsilon\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\\
&\mathrm{d}Y_{t}^{n+1}=-\bigl(Q^{\dagger}QX_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{n})\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+Z_{t}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}\mathrm{d}B_{t}+Z_{t}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&Y_{T}^{n+1}=R^{\dagger}RX_{T}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}R^{\dagger}g(\overline{m}^{n}_{T}).\end{split}

Importantly, we stress that, by construction,
{\bm{X}}_{\tau_{p}(\cdot)}^{n+1}
is
({\mathbb{F}}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}})_{0\leq t\leq T}
-adapted. We then let, for the same solution
{\bm{\eta}}^{(p)}
as in
(
2.34
)–(
2.36
),

(2.72)

\begin{split}\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}&={\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[Y_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]-\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}X^{n+1}_{\ell T/p}\\
&=Y_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}-\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}QX_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}^{n}_{\ell T/p})\Bigr)-\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}X^{n+1}_{\ell T/p},\end{split}

for

\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
.
We obtain, for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-2\}
,

\begin{split}&\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}-\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}=-\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}QX_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(\overline{m}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n}\bigr)\Bigr)-\tfrac{T}{p}\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\alpha^{n+1}_{\ell T/p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\Bigl(\eta_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}-\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr)X_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}+\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}k_{s}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}\mathrm{d}B_{s}+\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}k_{s}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{{\bm{h}}^{n}},\end{split}

for some square integrable and
{\mathbb{F}}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-progressively measurable processes
{\bm{k}}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}

and

{\bm{k}}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}
.
Above, we used the fact that
{W}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}^{p,{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}-{W}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{p,{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}={W}_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}-{W}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}
.
Now,
using
(
2.71
)
and conditioning on

{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}

in
(
2.72
), we obtain

\begin{split}\alpha_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}=-\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}X^{n+1}_{\ell T/p}-{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr],\quad\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\},\end{split}

which permits to write

(2.73)

\begin{split}X^{n+1}_{(\ell+1)T/p}&=X^{n+1}_{\ell T/p}+\tfrac{T}{p}\alpha_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}\sigma\Bigl(B_{(\ell+1)T/p}-B_{\ell T/p}\Bigr)+\varepsilon\Bigl(W^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}_{(\ell+1)T/p}-W^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}_{\ell T}\Bigr)\\
&=\Bigl(I_{d}-\tfrac{T}{p}\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr)X^{n+1}_{\ell T/p}-\tfrac{T}{p}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}\sigma\Bigl(B_{(\ell+1)T/p}-B_{\ell T/p}\Bigr)+\varepsilon\Bigl(W^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}_{(\ell+1)T/p}-W^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}_{\ell T}\Bigr).\end{split}

Modifying the processes
{\bm{k}}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}
and
{\bm{h}}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}

and
using in addition the first equation
for

{\bm{\eta}}^{(p)}
in (
2.34
), we
end-up with

(2.74)

\begin{split}\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}-\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}&=-\tfrac{T}{p\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n})+\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}+\rho_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr){\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}k_{s}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}\mathrm{d}B_{s}+\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}k_{s}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{{\bm{h}}^{n}},\end{split}

for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-2\}
, where

\rho_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}=\tfrac{T}{p\varpi}Q^{\dagger}Q+\Bigl(\eta_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}-\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr).

By
recalling that

\overline{m}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n}
is

{\mathcal{F}}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}}
-measurable and by
modifying the process

{\bm{k}}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}

accordingly,
we can rewrite
(
2.74
)
in the form

(2.75)

\begin{split}\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n+1}-\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}&=-\tfrac{T}{p}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\tfrac{1}{\varpi}Q^{\dagger}f(\overline{m}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{n})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(\eta_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}+\rho_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr){\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}|{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}k_{s}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}\mathrm{d}B_{s}+\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}k_{s}^{n+1,{\bm{W}}}\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{{\bm{h}}^{n}},\end{split}

for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-2\}
.
When
\ell=p-1
, we deduce
from
(
2.72
)
and
(
2.73
)
that

\begin{split}&\widetilde{h}^{n+1}_{(p-1)T/p}\\
&=R^{\dagger}R\,{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\bigl[X_{T}^{n+1}|{\mathcal{F}}_{(p-1)T/p}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\bigr]+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}R^{\dagger}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\bigl[g\bigl(\overline{m}^{n}_{T}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{(p-1)T/p}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\bigr]-\eta_{(p-1)T/p}^{(p)}X^{n+1}_{(p-1)T/p}\\
&=\Bigl[R^{\dagger}R\Bigl(I_{d}-\frac{T}{p}\eta_{(p-1)T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr)-\eta_{(p-1)T/p}^{(p)}\Bigr]X^{n+1}_{(p-1)T/p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\tfrac{1}{\varpi}R^{\dagger}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\bigl[g\bigl(\overline{m}^{n}_{T}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{(p-1)T/p}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\bigr]-\tfrac{T}{p}R^{\dagger}R\,{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\widetilde{h}_{(p-1)T/p}^{n+1}|{\mathcal{F}}_{(p-1)T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr],\end{split}

which yields, using the boundary condition in (
2.34
) together with the fact that

\overline{m}^{n}_{T}
is

{\mathcal{F}}_{T}^{{\bm{W}}}
-measurable

(2.76)

\widetilde{h}^{n+1}_{(p-1)T/p}=\tfrac{1}{\varpi}R^{\dagger}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\bigl[g\bigl(\overline{m}^{n}_{T}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{(p-1)T/p}^{{\bm{W}}}\bigr]-\tfrac{T}{p}R^{\dagger}R\,{\mathbb{E}}^{{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}}\Bigl[\widetilde{h}_{(p-1)T/p}^{n+1}|{\mathcal{F}}_{(p-1)T/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\Bigr].

We
now recall that, by construction,

\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}

is

{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-measurable.
Arguing as in Remark

2.16
, we can prove inductively (over
\ell
) that

\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}

is
in fact

{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}}
-measurable.
As a consequence,

{\bm{k}}^{n+1,{\bm{B}}}
has to be zero.
By combining
(
2.75
)
and
(
2.76
), we hence
recover BSDE (
2.38
).

∎

Proof of Lemma

2.22
.

The bound
(
2.58
) on
{\bm{h}}^{n}
is a direct consequence of
(
2.42
), which yields:

\begin{split}\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega\in\Omega}|\widetilde{h}^{n+1}_{\ell T/p}|\leq\bigl(1+\tfrac{C_{1}}{p}\bigr)\Bigl[\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega\in\Omega}|\widetilde{h}^{n+1}_{(\ell+1)T/p}|+\tfrac{C_{1}}{p}\Bigr],\quad\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\},\end{split}

for
C_{1}
as in the statement.
Observing that

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega\in\Omega}|\widetilde{h}^{n+1}_{T}|
is bounded by
C_{1}
, we get the result by means of
a straightforward backward induction. The bound on
\widetilde{\bm{h}}^{(p)}
is proven in a similar way.

In order to prove the second claim
in the statement (see (
2.59
)), we invoke
[
54
, Theorem 2.2]
.
Indeed, we notice that the
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}/\varepsilon}
-martingale

\biggl(\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}k_{s}^{(p)}\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}/\varepsilon}\biggr)_{0\leq t\leq T}

has a finite BMO norm, see for instance (2.1) with
p=2
in the book
[
54
]
by Kazamaki.
The proof is as follows. Rewriting (
2.56
) in the form

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}\widetilde{h}_{t}^{(p)}=\Bigl\{-{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}^{(p)}\bigr)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{p,{\bm{W}}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\Bigr]{\mathbf{1}}_{\{t\leq(p-1)T/p\}}\\
&\hskip 50.0pt+\Bigl(\eta_{\tau_{p(t)}+T/p}^{(p)}+\frac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}Q{\mathbf{1}}_{\{t\leq(p-1)T/p\}}\Bigr)h_{t}^{(p)}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon k_{t}^{(p)}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T),\\
&\widetilde{h}_{T}^{(p)}=R^{\dagger}g\bigl(m^{(p)}_{T}\bigr),\end{split}

we easily deduce that,
for any stopping

\tau
,

\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\int_{\tau}^{T}\varepsilon k_{s}^{(p)}\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\big|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{{\bm{W}}}\Bigr]\biggr|\leq C_{1},

for a possibly new value of
C_{1}
. We conclude by means of
[
54
, Theorem 2.2]
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.18
.

The equation for

(\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)})_{\ell=0,\cdots,p}
in (
2.46
) can be rewritten in
the form:

(2.77)

\begin{split}&\Bigl[I_{d}+\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(\eta_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}Q{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}\Bigr)\Bigr]\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\\
&={\mathbb{E}}\biggl[{\mathcal{E}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\bigr)\Bigl(\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}\Bigr)\,\bigl|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{\bm{W}}\biggr],\end{split}

for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
,
with the shorten notation

{\mathcal{E}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\bigr):=\exp\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\cdot\bigl(W_{(\ell+1)T/p}-W_{\ell T/p}\bigr)-\tfrac{T}{2\varepsilon^{2}p}|\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}|^{2}\Bigr).

The challenge is thus to
find, for each
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
,
a variable
\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\in L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})

that solves
(
2.77
),
when

\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}

is given
in
L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.

In order to do so, we consider the mapping

\begin{split}\Phi_{\ell}:L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})\ni y&\mapsto\Bigl[I_{d}+\tfrac{T}{p}\Bigl(\eta_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}Q{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}\Bigr)\Bigr]^{-1}\\
&\hskip 5.0pt\times{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[{\mathcal{E}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}y\bigr)\Bigl(\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}\Bigr)\,\bigl|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}}\biggr]\end{split}

and then look for a fixed point to it.

Following the second step
of Lemma

2.10
(see in particular (
2.28
)), we have, for any two

y,y^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,
with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}
,

\begin{split}&d_{\rm TV}\Bigl({\mathbb{P}}_{y}\bigl(\cdot\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,\bm{W}}\bigr),{\mathbb{P}}_{y^{\prime}}\bigl(\cdot\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,\bm{W}}\bigr)\Bigr)\leq\sqrt{2}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\tfrac{T}{p}|y-y^{\prime}|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq\bigl(\tfrac{T}{p\varepsilon^{2}}\bigr)^{1/2}|y-y^{\prime}|,\end{split}

where

{\mathbb{P}}_{y}={\mathcal{E}}(y)\cdot{\mathbb{P}}
(and similarly for
y^{\prime}
).
Above,

{\mathbb{P}}_{y}(\cdot\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,\bm{W}})

should be understood as a regular conditional probability
of
{\mathbb{P}}_{y}
on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{p,\bm{W}})

given

{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,\bm{W}}
(and similarly for
y^{\prime}
).

Recalling that
\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}+[T/p]Q^{\dagger}f(m_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}
is bounded (by a constant independent of
\varepsilon
,
\ell
and
p
),
we deduce that
there exists a constant
c
(depending on the same parameters as those quoted in the statement)
such that, with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}
,

|\Phi_{\ell}(y^{\prime})-\Phi_{\ell}(y)|\leq c\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{p}\varepsilon}|y^{\prime}-y|.

And then,

{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|\Phi_{\ell}(y^{\prime})-\Phi_{\ell}(y)|^{2}\bigr]\leq\tfrac{c^{2}}{p\varepsilon^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|y^{\prime}-y|^{2}\bigr],

which proves that
\Phi_{\ell}
is a contraction in

L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{p,{\bm{W}}},{\mathbb{P}};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})

when
\sqrt{p}\varepsilon>c
.
By (backward) induction on
\ell
(following the proof of Lemma

2.22
), we know that
each
\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}

by a constant
C

(depending on the same parameters as those quoted in the statement).

Once

\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}
has been found in
(
2.46
), it remains to find
the process
(k_{s}^{(p)})_{\ell T/p\leq s\leq(\ell+1)T/p}
. This can be done by solving the following standard BSDE
(with the pair process
({\bm{Y}},{\bm{Z}})=(Y_{s},Z_{s})_{\ell T/p\leq s\leq(\ell+1)T/p}
as unknown, taking values in

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
):

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}Y_{s}=-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}\mathrm{d}s+\Bigl(\eta_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}+\tfrac{T}{p}Q^{\dagger}Q{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\ell\leq p-2\}}\Bigr)\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+Z_{s}\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}\mathrm{d}s+\varepsilon Z_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s},\end{split}

for
s\in[\ell T/p,(\ell+1)T/p]
, with the boundary condition
Y_{(\ell+1)T/p}=\widetilde{h}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{(p)}
(at time
(\ell+1)T/p
).
Existence and uniqueness of a solution is standard because

\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}
is in
L^{\infty}
.
Following
the last sentence in Remark

2.16

(about measurability of the conditioning
of an

{\mathcal{F}}_{(\ell+1)T/p}^{p,\bm{W}}
-measurable random variable
given

{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{\bm{W}}
),
we deduce that
Y_{\ell T/p}
coincides with

\widetilde{h}_{\ell T/p}^{(p)}
and eventually

(Z_{s})_{\ell T/p\leq s\leq(\ell+1)T/p}

solves the equation with
(k_{s}^{(p)})_{\ell T/p\leq s\leq(\ell+1)T/p}

as unknown in
(
2.46
). Uniqueness of this choice
can be easily checked by observing (from Itô’s isometry) that, for another
solution, say
(k_{s}^{(p),\prime})_{\ell T/p\leq s\leq(\ell+1)T/p}
,

\begin{split}\varepsilon^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl|k_{s}^{(p)}-k_{s}^{(p),\prime}\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}s&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl(k_{s}^{(p)}-k_{s}^{(p),\prime}\bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\\
&\leq\tfrac{CT}{p}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl|k_{s}^{(p)}-k_{s}^{(p),\prime}\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\biggr],\end{split}

for a possibly new choice of the constant
C
. Uniqueness easily follows
if
CT/p<\varepsilon^{2}
.
∎

2.3.
Exploitation versus exploration

We now address the exploitability, when we play the equilibrium strategy given by the
fictitious play.

2.3.1.
Approximate Nash equilibrium formed by
the solution of the MFG with common noise

We first prove (which is easier) that the
solution of the

p
-discrete MFG with common noise
(as defined in the statement of Theorem

2.17
and in
(
2.52
)–(
2.55
))
forms an approximate Nash equilibrium of the game without common noise.
In order to do so, we recall
from
(
2.53
)
that

{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star}
is the
equilibrium strategy of the

p
-discrete MFG associated with
the cost functional
(
2.52
)
and that the optimal trajectory is given by
(
2.54
).

We claim:

Proposition 2.24
.

There exists a constant
C
, only depending on the parameters
d
,
T
,
\|f\|_{1,\infty}
,
\|g\|_{1,\infty}
,

|Q|
,
|R|
and
{\mathbb{E}}[|X_{0}|^{2}]
, such that, for any integer
p\geq 1
,

\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]-C\varepsilon,

the infimum being taken over

{\mathbb{F}}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}=(\sigma(X_{0},(B_{\tau_{p}(s)},W_{\tau_{p}(s)})_{s\leq t}))_{0\leq t\leq T}
-progressively measurable
and square-integrable process

({\bm{\alpha}}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
that is piecewise constant on any interval
[\ell T/p,(\ell+1)T/p)
, for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
.

We start with the following lemma:

Lemma 2.25
.

There exists a constant
C
, only depending on
d
,
T
,
\|f\|_{1,\infty}
,
\|g\|_{1,\infty}
,

|Q|
and
|R|
, such that, for any integer
p\geq 1
and any

{\mathbb{F}}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-progressively measurable
and square-integrable process

{\bm{\alpha}}=({\alpha}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
that is piecewise constant on any interval
[\ell T/p,(\ell+1)T/p)
, for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
,

\begin{split}&\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};\varepsilon{\bm{W}}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)\Bigr]-{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\biggr|\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\leq C\biggl(1+{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|X_{0}|^{2}\bigr]^{1/2}+{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]^{1/2}\biggr)\varepsilon.\end{split}

The symbol
{\mathbb{E}}
instead of

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
for the
L^{2}
moment of the initial condition is
fully justified by the fact that
X_{0}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
-measurable.

Proof.

For the same initial condition
X_{0}
and the same
{\bm{\alpha}}
as in the statement, we let

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{(p),\varepsilon}=\alpha_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathrm{d}B_{t}+\varepsilon\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T].\end{split}

In particular, we write
{\bm{X}}^{(p),0}=(X_{t}^{(p),0})_{0\leq t\leq T}

when there is no common noise. Then, we obviously have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|X_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}}-X_{t}^{0}|\leq C\varepsilon\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|W_{t}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}|\leq C\varepsilon\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|W_{t}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}|.

Meanwhile, we observe that

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigl(|X_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}}|^{2}+|X_{t}^{0}|^{2}\bigr)\Bigr]\leq C\biggl(1+{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[|X_{0}|^{2}\bigr]+{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\biggr).

Recalling
(
2.33
),
we get the result.
∎

The next lemma says that we can easily restrict ourselves to controls with a finite energy.

Lemma 2.26
.

There exists a constant
A
, only depending on the parameters
d
,
T
,
\|f\|_{1,\infty}
,

\|g\|_{1,\infty}
,
|Q|
,
|R|

and
{\mathbb{E}}[|X_{0}|^{2}]

such that, for any

\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, any integer
p\geq 1
and any

{\mathbb{F}}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-progressively measurable
and square-integrable process

{\bm{\alpha}}=({\alpha}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
that is piecewise constant on any interval
[\ell T/p,(\ell+1)T/p)
, for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\geq A\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\Rightarrow{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl(0;{\bm{m}}^{(p)};\varepsilon{\bm{W}}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)\Bigr]+1.\end{split}

Proof.

It suffices to observe that

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr].

Meanwhile,

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl(0;{\bm{m}}^{(p)};\varepsilon{\bm{W}}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)\Bigr]\leq C.

The proof is easily completed.
∎

We now complete the

Proof of Proposition
2.24
.

By Lemma
2.26
,
we can reduce ourselves to
the set
{\mathcal{E}}_{A}
of processes
{\bm{\alpha}}
(as in the statement of Proposition
2.24
) such that

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\leq A.

We then apply
Lemma

2.25
, which says that

\sup_{{\bm{\alpha}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{A}}\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};\varepsilon{\bm{W}}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)\Bigr]-{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}};0\bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq C\varepsilon.

The proof is easily completed, using
(
2.52
)–(
2.53
),
from which we get that, for any

{\bm{\alpha}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{A}
,

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};\varepsilon{\bm{W}}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)\Bigr]&\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};\varepsilon{\bm{W}}^{p,{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]+C\varepsilon.\end{split}

This completes the proof.
∎

2.3.2.
Approximate Nash equilibrium formed by the return of the fictitious play

We now state a similar result, but for the approximation returned by the fictitious play
subjected to the
\varepsilon
-randomization and to the
p
-discretization.
Throughout this paragraph,

{\bm{h}}^{n+1}
and

{\bm{m}}^{n+1}
are as in
(
2.43
)
and
(
2.44
). We recall that these two processes depend on
p
(although it is not indicated in the notation).

The point is then to consider
the strategy
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}
defined by

(2.78)

\alpha_{t}^{(p),n,\diamond}:=-\eta_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}X_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),\star}-\varpi h_{t}^{n},

where we recall
(see
(
2.54
))

(2.79)

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}X^{(p),\star}_{t}&=-\eta_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}X_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),\star}\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathrm{d}B_{t}+\varepsilon\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{p}\\
&=\alpha_{t}^{(p),n,\diamond}\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathrm{d}B_{t}+\varepsilon\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{p,\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T];\quad X_{0}^{(p),n,\diamond}=X_{0}.\end{split}

Following
(
2.44
),
we have

(2.80)

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[X_{t}^{(p),\star}\,|\,\sigma({\bm{W}})\Bigr]={\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[X_{t}^{(p),\star}\,|\,\sigma({\bm{W}})\Bigr]=m_{t}^{(p)},\quad t\in[0,T].

In other words, the conditional state under the candidate strategy
is the environment itself, which is a pre-requisite in mean field games.
Notice that

{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}

and
{\bm{h}}^{n}
and do depend on
\varepsilon
.

Theorem 2.27
.

Assume that the law of the initial condition has sub-Gaussian tails, i.e.

{\mathbb{P}}(\{|X_{0}|\geq r\})\leq a^{-1}\exp(-ar^{2})
, for some

a>0
and for any
r>0
. Then,
there exist two positive constants
c
and
C
, only depending on the parameters
a
,
d
,
T
,
\|f\|_{1,\infty}
,
\|g\|_{1,\infty}
,

|Q|
and
|R|
, such that, for any

\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, any integer
p\geq 1
satisfying
p\varepsilon^{2}\geq c

and any integer
n\geq 1
,

(2.81)

\begin{split}\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-C\varepsilon-Cp^{-1}-\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n},\end{split}

the infimum in the left-hand side being taken over

{\mathbb{F}}^{X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-progressively measurable
and square-integrable processes

({\bm{\alpha}}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
.

The difference between the term in the left-hand side and the first term in the right-hand side of
(
2.81
) is called the

{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
-mean exploitability of the policy
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}
. (Obviously, this difference is non-positive.)

We feel useful to comment on the meaning and scope of Theorem
2.27
. We first observe that, in the
functional
{\mathcal{R}}
that appears in
{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)

and
{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)
, on both sides of the main inequality
(
2.81
), there is no time discretization and no common noise (since the third input is
0
). In other words, both the costs to

({\bm{\alpha}},{\bm{m}}^{(p)})

and

({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond},{\bm{m}}^{(p)})

are computed according to the time-continuous original model without common noise (even though the control
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}
is piecewise constant and random as an output of the scheme). In particular, for a given realization of the common noise
{\bm{W}}

(which does manifest here because
{\bm{\alpha}}
,
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}
,
{\bm{m}}^{(p)}
and
{\bm{h}}^{n}

depend on
{\bm{W}}
), the conditional expectations

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)\,|\,\sigma({\bm{W}})]

and

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)\,|\,\sigma({\bm{W}})]

coincide with the
costs

J({\bm{\alpha}}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}});{\bm{m}}^{(p)}({\bm{W}}))

and

J({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}});{\bm{m}}^{(p)}({\bm{W}}))
, for

J
as
in
(
1.2
) (with the expectation in the latter being just performed over
(X_{0},{\bm{B}})
).
In particular, the notations

{\bm{\alpha}}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}})
,

{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}})

and

{\bm{m}}^{(p)}({\bm{W}})
are used here to emphasize that, in the computation of
J
, the realization of
the common noise is kept frozen
in the inputs

{\bm{\alpha}}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}})
,

{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}})

and

{\bm{m}}^{(p)}({\bm{W}})
.
In turn, the two expectations in (
2.81
)
can be rewritten as

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}[J({\bm{\alpha}}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}});{\bm{m}}^{(p)}(\cdot,{\bm{W}}))]

and

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}[J({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}(\cdot,\cdot,{\bm{W}});{\bm{m}}^{(p)}(\cdot,{\bm{W}}))]
,
with the expectation
{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
being now taken under the sole common noise.
This can be rephrased quite simply:
Theorem

2.27

provides a bound for the
{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
-mean exploitability associated with the original MFG,
the mean being here taken with respect to
the tilted law of the common noise (i.e., the law of
{\bm{W}}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
).
The bound that is given for the mean exploitability depends on the three parameters
\varepsilon
,
n
and
p
. Typically, we want to choose

\varepsilon
small and
p
large, which is well understood: the equilibrium
({\bm{m}}^{(p)},{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond})

is learnt for the
p
-discrete MFG with an
\varepsilon
-common noise; if
\varepsilon
is large or
p
is small, the equilibrium that is learnt cannot be
a ‘good’ approximate equilibrium of the original MFG. This is exactly what the terms
-C\varepsilon
and
-C/p
say in
(
2.81
).
As for the last term in
(
2.81
), it
becomes smaller and smaller as
n
increases.
This is also consistent with the intuition: the mean exploitability becomes small when the number

n
of iterations of the fictitious play is large.
For sure, there is a price to pay: as
\varepsilon
tends
0
, the impact of the common noise becomes smaller and

n
has to be chosen larger to attain the same accuracy for the mean exploitability.

The reader must also realize that, differently from what is done
in (
2.32
), the environment used in the cost functional
is the conditional state of the
reference particle when using the strategy
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond}
. This looks subtle, but
this makes a big difference in the analysis.
Indeed, if we had to
follow
(
2.32
)
and thus
use
\overline{\bm{m}}^{n}
as environment in
the cost functional, then
the
resulting minimizing path
would NOT be typical of the
environment, meaning that its (theoretical) conditional expectation
(under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
or
{\mathbb{P}}
)
given the common noise would NOT match

\overline{\bm{m}}^{n}
.
Differently, our choice
to use
{\bm{m}}^{(p)}
as environment in the
statement of Theorem

2.27

allows for the following property:
the
dynamics
(
2.79
)
are typical of the environment, meaning that
the environment is exactly given by the conditional state given
the realization of the common noise, which is exactly what
(
2.80
) says.
In this framework,
Theorem

2.27
asserts that, by deviating unilaterally from the rest of the population,
a reference player could hardly
increase her cost, at best by a remainder that
is small when
p
and
n
are large and
\varepsilon
is fixed.

We end-up this discussion with the following two observations, which may echo possible questions of the reader.
First, one may wonder about the practical implementation of
(
2.79
), as it requires to know the value of

{\bm{\eta}}^{(p)}
. In fact, as it is clarified in the next section, any efficient method
that would be used in the implementation of the fictitious play
should learn, at the same time, the solution
to the Riccati equation (
2.34
), even though the coefficients
f
and
g
are not known. This is somehow a pre-requisite
in the implementation of the fictitious play. Second, the fact that the dynamics
(
2.79
)
are independent of

n
(under the historical measure) may be rather intriguing. Of course, one must recall in this regard that, on a statistical point of view, the dynamics that truly matter are
in fact
those computed under the tilted probability measure. Indeed, the two costs
in
(
2.81
)
are averaged under the tilted measure. Should we represent those costs in terms of an infinite cloud of particles,
then all the particles forming the approximate Nash equilibrium
provided by
Theorem

2.27

should be subjected to a common noise that would be sampled under the tilted probability measure.
This is exactly the point where the dependence on
n
manifests.

We start with the following
refinement of
Theorem

2.17
:

Lemma 2.28
.

With the same notation as in the statement of Theorem

2.17
,
there exist two positive constants
c
and
C
, only depending on the parameters
a
,
d
,
T
,
\|f\|_{1,\infty}
,
\|g\|_{1,\infty}
,

|Q|
and
|R|
, such that, for any

\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, any integer
p\geq 1
satisfying
p\varepsilon^{2}\geq c

and any integer
n\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\alpha_{t}^{(p),n,\diamond}-\alpha_{t}^{(p),\star}|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\varpi^{-2n}\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2}),\end{split}

where we recall
that

{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star}
is the
equilibrium strategy of the

p
-discrete
MFG with an
\varepsilon
common noise, as given by
(
2.53
).

Proof of Lemma
2.28
.

We recall
from
(
2.53
)
and
(
2.78
)
that

\alpha_{t}^{(p),n,\diamond}-\alpha_{t}^{(p),\star}=\varpi h^{n}_{t}-h^{(p)}_{t},\quad t\in[0,T],

and the bound in the statement follows
from Theorem

2.17
.
∎

We now
turn to

Proof of Theorem
2.27
.

First Step.

Recalling the shape of the optimizer
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n+1,\diamond}

in
(
2.78
)–(
2.79
)
, together with
the fact that
{\bm{h}}^{n+1}

is bounded, uniformly in
n\geq 1
, see
Lemma
2.22
(recalling that
C_{1}
therein is independent of
n
, as mentioned in the statement), we deduce that

\Bigl|{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr|\leq C,

where the constant
C
is independent
n
. In the rest of the proof, the value of
C
may vary from line to line provided it remains independent
of
n
. Then, we must invoke the fact that the problem

\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]

is a linear quadratic-problem in a random environment.
Similar to the minimization problem
studied in Lemma
2.1
, its solution is given in feedback form. If we call
\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{n+1}
the minimizer, it reads

(2.82)

\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{n}=-\bigl(\eta_{t}\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}+\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}\bigr),\quad t\in[0,T],

with

(2.83)

\mathrm{d}\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}=-\bigl(\eta_{t}\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}+\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad t\in[0,T];\quad\widehat{X}_{0}^{n}=X_{0},

and where

(2.84)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}=\Bigl(-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{t}\bigr)+\eta_{t}\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon\widehat{k}_{t}^{n}\mathrm{d}W_{t}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&\widehat{h}_{T}^{n}=R^{\dagger}g\bigl(m^{(p)}_{T}\bigr).\end{split}

We start with a series of results whose proof is given in the last step below.
We first claim that, for each
k\in\{0,\cdots,p\}
,

\widehat{h}_{kT/p}^{n}
is

{\mathcal{F}}_{kT/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}
-measurable.
Moreover,

(2.85)

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega\in\Omega}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}|\leq C.

As a result, with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}

(and also under
{\mathbb{P}}
),

(2.86)

\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}|\leq C\Bigl(1+|X_{0}|+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|B_{t}|\Bigr),\\
&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\widehat{\alpha}_{t}^{n}|\leq C\Bigl(1+|X_{0}|+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|B_{t}|\Bigr),\end{split}

which implies

(2.87)

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigl|\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\leq C.

and

(2.88)

{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\leq C\Bigl(1+|X_{0}|^{2}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|B_{t}|^{2}\Bigr).

Second Step.

By the first step,

\begin{split}\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]&={\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr].\end{split}

Here,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&=\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[|R^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{T}^{n}+g(m_{T}^{(p)})|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\{|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}+f(m_{t}^{(p)})|^{2}+|\widehat{\alpha}^{n}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t\biggr],\end{split}

and the purpose is to lower bound the above cost by
{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)

for some well-chosen

{\mathbb{F}}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-progressively measurable
and piecewise constant
control
\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n}
and with

{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}
as in
(
2.33
). In order to do so, we let

(2.89)

\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}:=\tfrac{p}{T}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{\tau_{p}(t)+T/p}\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}\biggr],\quad t\in[0,T],

which allows us to define by induction:

(2.90)

\widehat{X}_{(k+1)T/p}^{(p),n}:=\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\tfrac{T}{p}\eta_{kT/p}\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\tfrac{T}{p}\widehat{h}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}+\sigma\bigl(B_{(k+1)T/p}-B_{kT/p}\bigr),

for

k\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
(with
X_{0}
as initial condition).
We prove in the fourth step below
that

(2.91)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\sup_{k=0,\cdots,p}\bigl|\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}-\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\tfrac{C}{p},\end{split}

and

(2.92)

\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\bigl|\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}-\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\tfrac{C}{p},\end{split}

for a constant
C
as in the statement of
Theorem
2.27
.
By
(
2.40
), we then also have

(2.93)

\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\bigl|\eta_{t}\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}-\eta_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\tfrac{C}{p},\end{split}

Together with
(
2.87
), we deduce from the latter displays that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}+f(m^{(p)}_{t})|^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}+f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)}\bigr)|^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 5.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\Bigl(|Q^{\dagger}\bigl(\widehat{X}_{t}^{n}-\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}\bigr)|+|f(m_{t}^{(p)})-f(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)})|\Bigr)\,|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}+f(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)})|\Bigr]

(2.94)

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}+f(m^{(p)}_{\tau_{p}(t)})|^{2}\Bigr]-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.

Proceeding similarly for the other terms in the cost functional, we obtain

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]&\geq\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[|R^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{T}^{(p),n}+g(m_{T}^{(p)})|^{2}\\
&\hskip-40.0pt+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\{|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}+f(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)})|^{2}+|\eta_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\widehat{X}^{(p),n}_{\tau_{p}(t)}+\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}|^{2}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}},\end{split}

and by
conditioning
the last term in the integral
by

{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
,
we deduce from
(
2.89
)
and
Jensen’s inequality that

(2.95)

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]&\geq\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[|R^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{T}^{(p),n}+g(m_{T}^{(p)})|^{2}\\
&\hskip-30.0pt+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\{|Q^{\dagger}\widehat{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n}+f(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)})|^{2}+|\widehat{\alpha}^{(p),n}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\widehat{\alpha}^{(p),n}_{t}&=-\eta_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)}\widehat{X}^{(p),n}_{\tau_{p}(t)}-\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),n},\end{split}

for
t\in[0,T]
.
Clearly, the above conditioning is licit because
the process
(\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n})_{k=0,\cdots,p}

is

({\mathcal{F}}_{kT/p}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}})_{k=0,\cdots,p}
-progressively measurable.
By the way, we deduce
from the latter that the process

(\widehat{\alpha}^{(p),n}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

is

{\mathbb{F}}^{p,X_{0},{\bm{B}},{\bm{W}}}
-progressively measurable, square-integrable
and
piecewise constant on any interval
[\ell T/p,(\ell+1)T/p)
, for
\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}
.
In turn, the right-hand side in
(
2.95
)
can be rewritten

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.

We deduce that, for any
\varrho>1
,

\begin{split}&\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{{p,X_{0}}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{{\mathcal{R}}^{{p,X_{0}}}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)\leq\varrho\}}\Bigr]-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.\end{split}

Using
the upper bound on
d_{\textrm{\rm TV}}\bigl({\mathbb{P}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon},{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigr)
(see Remark
2.20
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 5.0pt\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{{p,X_{0}}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{{\mathcal{R}}^{{p},X_{0}}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)\leq\varrho\}}\Bigr]-\varrho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.\end{split}

And then, by
(
2.88
) and the sub-Gaussian property of the law of
X_{0}
,
we obtain

(2.96)

\begin{split}\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{{p,X_{0}}}\bigl(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)-\varrho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}},\end{split}

for some
\eta>0
.
Finally, by
Proposition

2.24
,

(2.97)

\displaystyle\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]-C\varepsilon-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)-\rho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.

Third Step.

We now revert the computations achieved in the second step.
Following
(
2.95
)
and then
(
2.94
) and the proof of Lemma

2.25
, the cost on the last line
can be lower bounded as follows:

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{p,X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&=\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[|R^{\dagger}X_{T}^{(p),\star,0}+g(m_{T}^{(p)})|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\{|Q^{\dagger}{X}_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p),\star,0}+f(m_{\tau_{p}(t)}^{(p)})|^{2}+|{\alpha}^{(p),\star}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\\
&\geq\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[|R^{\dagger}X_{T}^{(p),\star}+g(m_{T}^{(p)})|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\{|Q^{\dagger}{X}_{t}^{(p),\star}+f(m_{t}^{(p)})|^{2}+|{\alpha}^{(p),\star}_{t}|^{2}\Bigr\}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}-C\varepsilon,\end{split}

where
{\bm{X}}^{(p),\star,0}
on the second line denotes the time-continuous continuous process defined by

\begin{split}{X}^{(p),\star,0}_{t}&={X}^{(p),\star,0}_{kT/p}+\bigl(t-\tfrac{kT}{p}\bigr){\alpha}_{kT/p}^{(p),\star}+\sigma\bigl(B_{t}-B_{kT/p}\bigr),\quad t\in[\tfrac{kT}{p},\tfrac{(k+1)T}{p}],\quad k\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}.\end{split}

In particular,

{\bm{X}}^{(p),\star,0}
is the time-continuous process driven
by
{\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star}
when there is no common noise.
Notice that

{\bm{X}}^{(p),\star,0}
differs from
the process

{\bm{X}}^{(p),\star}

that appears on the third line and that is defined
in
(
2.54
).

In turn,
by Proposition

2.28

and for
\rho
as in
(
2.97
),

\begin{split}&\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),\star};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]-C\varepsilon-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)-\rho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}\\
&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]-C\varepsilon-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)-\rho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}\\
&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\bm{h}}^{(p)}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)\leq\varrho\}}\Bigr]-C\varepsilon-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\rho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.\end{split}

And then, as in the derivation of
(
2.96
),

\begin{split}&\inf_{\bm{\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0)\leq\varrho\}}\Bigr]-C\varepsilon-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\rho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}\\
&\geq{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[{\mathcal{R}}^{X_{0}}\bigl({\bm{\alpha}}^{(p),n,\diamond};{\bm{m}}^{(p)};0\bigr)\Bigr]-C\varepsilon-C\exp\bigl(-\eta\varrho\bigr)-\rho\exp(C\varepsilon^{-2})\varpi^{-n}-\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{p}}.\end{split}

Choosing

\eta\varrho=-\ln(\varepsilon)

and modifying the value of
C
,
we complete the proof.

Fourth Step.

It now remains to prove some auxiliary results that are used in the previous steps.

We start with the analysis of

\widehat{\bm{h}}^{n}

in
(
2.84
).
We recall from
(
2.43
) that,
that, for each
t\in[0,T]
,

{h}_{t}^{n}
is

(\sigma((W^{p}_{k})_{k\leq\lfloor tp/T\rfloor T/p}))_{0\leq t\leq T}
-measurable.
Hence, from
(
2.78
)
and
(
2.79
),
each

{m}_{t}^{(p)}
is

(\sigma((W^{p}_{k})_{k\leq\lceil tp/T\rceil T/p}))_{0\leq t\leq T}
-measurable (notice the use of the ceil part instead of the floor part in the time index of the filtration).
Further, we notice from
(
2.84
)
and
Remark

2.7

that

\begin{split}&P_{kT/p}\widehat{h}_{kT/p}^{n}={\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[P_{T}R^{\dagger}g\bigl(m^{(p)}_{T}\bigr)+\int_{kT/p}^{T}P_{s}Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{t}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}^{\bm{W}}_{kT/p}\biggr],\quad k\in\{0,\cdots,p\}.\end{split}

Following the analysis of
(
2.42
), the left-hand side is

{\mathcal{F}}_{kT/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}
-measurable. Noting
that each
P_{t}
, for
t\in[0,T]
, is invertible, we deduce that

\widehat{h}_{kT/p}^{n}

is

{\mathcal{F}}_{kT/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}
-measurable, which property is used below.

And then,
(
2.85
) follows from the above representation of

(P_{kT/p}\widehat{h}_{kT/p}^{n})_{k=0,\cdots,p}
(with a similar representation
of

P_{t}\widehat{h}_{t}^{n}

for any
t\in[0,T]
). The two bounds in
(
2.86
)
easily follow.

We
now turn to the proof of
(
2.91
) and
(
2.92
). By
(
2.90
) ,
we know that

\widehat{X}_{(k+1)T/p}^{(p),n}=\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\tfrac{T}{p}\eta_{kT/p}\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\tfrac{T}{p}\widehat{h}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}+\sigma\bigl(B_{(k+1)T/p}-B_{kT/p}\bigr),\quad k\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}.

Meanwhile,
recalling (
2.83
), we write

\begin{split}\widehat{X}_{(k+1)T/p}^{n}&=\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}-\int_{kT/p}^{(k+1)T/p}\bigl[\eta_{s}\widehat{X}_{s}^{n}+\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s+\sigma\bigl(B_{(k+1)T/p}-B_{kT/p}\bigr)\\
&=\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}-\biggl(\tfrac{T}{p}\eta_{kT/p}\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}+\int_{kT/p}^{(k+1)T/p}\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\mathrm{d}s\biggr)-\tfrac{T}{p}\widehat{A}_{k}^{n}+\sigma\bigl(B_{(k+1)T/p}-B_{kT/p}\bigr),\end{split}

with

(2.98)

\begin{split}\widehat{A}_{k}^{n}=\tfrac{p}{T}\int_{kT/p}^{(k+1)T/p}\bigl[\eta_{s}\widehat{X}_{s}^{n}-\eta_{kT/p}\widehat{X}^{n}_{kT/p}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

And then,

\begin{split}\widehat{X}_{(k+1)T/p}^{(p),n}-\widehat{X}_{(k+1)T/p}^{n}&=\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}-\tfrac{T}{p}\eta_{kT/p}\bigl(\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}\bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{kT/p}^{(k+1)T/p}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(s)}^{(p),n}-\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s+\tfrac{T}{p}\widehat{A}_{k}^{n}.\end{split}

By discrete Gronwall’s lemma, there exists a constant
C
(depending on the same parameters as in the statement of
Theorem
2.27
) such that

\sup_{\ell=0,\cdots,p}\bigl|\widehat{X}_{\ell T/p}^{(p),n}-\widehat{X}_{\ell T/p}^{n+1}\bigr|\leq C\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\biggl(\biggl|\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(s)}^{(p),n}-\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\biggr|+\tfrac{T}{p}\bigl|\widehat{A}_{\ell}^{n}\bigr|\biggr).

And then,

(2.99)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\sup_{k=0,\cdots,p}\bigl|\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\\
&\leq C\tfrac{T}{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\tfrac{p^{2}}{T^{2}}\biggl|\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(s)}^{(p),n}-\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\biggr|^{2}+\bigl|\widehat{A}_{\ell}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\biggr].\end{split}

Here, we observe
from
(
2.89
) that

(2.100)

\begin{split}&\tfrac{T}{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\tfrac{p^{2}}{T^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(s)}^{(p),n}-\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\\
&=\tfrac{p}{T}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}\biggr]-\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\mathrm{d}s\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\\
&\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\bigl|\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}-{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}\bigr]\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

By
(
2.84
), we have, for

\ell\in\{0,\cdots,p-1\}

and for
s\in[\ell T/p,(\ell+1)T/p]
,

\begin{split}\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}=\widehat{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n}+\int_{\ell T/p}^{s}\bigl[-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{r}\bigr)+\eta_{r}\widehat{h}_{r}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}r+\varepsilon\int_{\ell T/p}^{s}\widehat{k}_{r}^{n}\mathrm{d}W_{r}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}.\end{split}

Taking the conditional expectation given
{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}
under
{\mathbb{P}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}\bigr]=\widehat{h}_{\ell T/p}^{n}+{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\int_{\ell T/p}^{s}\bigl[-Q^{\dagger}f\bigl(m^{(p)}_{r}\bigr)+\eta_{r}\widehat{h}_{r}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}r\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}\biggr],\end{split}

and then

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\bigl|\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}-{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\ell T/p}^{{\bm{W}}^{p}}\bigr]\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\tfrac{C}{p^{2}}+C\varepsilon^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl(\int_{\ell T/p}^{s}|\widehat{k}_{r}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr).\end{split}

Finally,
by
(
2.100
),

\begin{split}&\tfrac{T}{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\tfrac{p^{2}}{T^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}\bigl[\widehat{h}_{\tau_{p}(s)}^{(p),n}-\widehat{h}_{s}^{n}\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\\
&\leq\tfrac{C}{p^{2}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{p-1}\int_{\ell T/p}^{(\ell+1)T/p}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl(\int_{\ell T/p}^{s}\varepsilon^{2}|\widehat{k}_{r}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq\tfrac{C}{p^{2}}+\tfrac{C}{p}{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon^{2}|\widehat{k}_{r}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr).\end{split}

Taking the square in
(
2.84
)
and using
(
2.85
), we can prove that

{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon^{2}|\widehat{k}_{r}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr)\leq C.

Back to
(
2.98
)
and (
2.99
), we get

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{\varpi{\bm{h}}^{n}\hskip-2.0pt/\varepsilon}\Bigl[\sup_{k=0,\cdots,p}\bigl|\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{(p),n}-\widehat{X}_{kT/p}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\tfrac{C}{p},\end{split}

which is
(
2.91
).

By
(
2.83
), we easily obtain
(
2.92
).
∎

3.
Numerical experiments

This section is devoted to several numerical experiments
based on our learning algorithm. We first provide
in Subsection
3.1

a theoretical analysis of a benchmark example that we use throughout the section.
In Subsection
3.2
, we present several variants of the implemented version of the algorithm. We also explain how to compute a reference solution.
Then, we
study in Subsection
3.3
the numerical behavior of the algorithm
when the
intensity
\varepsilon
of the common noise is equal to 1.
In Subsection
3.4
, we explain some of the difficulties that arise in the large dimension setting.
Finally, in
Subsection

3.5
, we address the small viscosity regime.
In both Subsections

3.3

and

3.5
, we discuss the influence of the parameter
\varpi
. In particular, we compare the two harmonic (
\varpi=1
) and
geometric (
\varpi>1
) versions of the algorithm. In this regard, it is worth recalling Remark

2.6
: all our results hold for the harmonic version of the
fictitious play with the geometric decay
\varpi^{-n}
being replaced by
1/n
.

3.1.
A benchmark example

As a particular instance of the cost functional
J
in
(
1.2
), we focus here on

(3.1)

J\bigl({\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{m}}\bigr)=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\bigl|X_{T}+g(m_{T})\bigr|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr],

which implicitly means that
f=0
.
The examples that are addressed below are in dimension
d=1
,
d=2
,
d=12
and
d=20
, with
T=1
in any cases.
For simplicity, we also work with
X_{0}=0
.

In dimension
d=1
, we choose

(3.2)

g(x)=\cos(\kappa x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}},

for a free positive parameter
\kappa
that we tune from smaller to larger values.
Obviously, the Lipschitz constant of
g
becomes larger with
\kappa
. Accordingly, the coupling between the two forward and backward equations in
(
1.7
) becomes stronger as
\kappa
gets larger, which makes it more difficult to solve, especially when
\varepsilon=0
. We illustrate this in Lemma
3.1
below: it says that, when
\varepsilon=0
,
(
1.7
) is uniquely solvable when
|\kappa|<2
. Even more, the analysis performed in

[
25
]
shows that the more standard (and obviously simpler) Picard scheme
(
1.9
)–(
1.10
)
would converge when
\kappa
is small, whether there is a common noise or not. Our result is thus especially relevant when
\kappa
gets larger.

In dimension
2
, we work with a similar terminal cost
g=(g_{1},g_{2})
:

(3.3)

\begin{split}&g_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})=\cos(\kappa x_{1})\cos(\kappa x_{2}),\quad g_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})=\sin(\kappa x_{1})\sin(\kappa x_{2}),\quad x_{1},\ x_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}},\end{split}

where, as in dimension 1,
\kappa
is a free positive parameter that we tune from smaller to larger values.

As we just said, we also address higher dimensional examples, with
d=12
or
d=20
. In order to encode the function
g
in a systematic manner, we
then choose:

(3.4)

\begin{split}&g_{i}(x)=\cos\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Theta_{i,j}x_{j}\biggr)\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\quad i=1,\cdots,d,\end{split}

for a fixed square matrix
\Theta
of size
d\times d
, whose choice depends on the examples.
For instance, we may take
\Theta_{i,j}=(i+j)/(2d)
. The impact of
\Theta
is then quite similar to the impact of

\kappa
in the low dimensional case as it induces highly oscillatory phenomena, which make the coupling in
(
1.7
) stronger.

Regardless of the choice of
g
,
the Riccati equation
(
2.3
)
associated with
(
3.1
) writes

(3.5)

\dot{\eta}_{t}-\eta_{t}^{2}=0,\quad t\in[0,1]\ ;\quad\eta_{1}=1,

the solution of which is given by

(3.6)

\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{2-t},\quad t\in[0,1].

Obviously, the
solution to (
2.3
) is here identified with a scalar-valued function while, formally, it takes values in
the set of square
d\times d
matrices.
This follows from the fact that
Q
and
R
in
(
2.3
) are just the identity matrix.
Numerically, this makes both the code and the analysis slightly easier, but the resulting restriction on the scope of the results is in fact limited,
the real challenge being to learn
{\bm{h}}^{n}
in
(
2.32
).

3.1.1.
Solutions without common noise

Even though this is not needed for all the examples we handle below, we feel useful
to have a preliminary discussion about the shape of the solutions when there is no common noise, keeping in mind that, originally, the true model of interest is the MFG without common noise. Recalling (
1.7
),
we
indeed have that,
whenever there is no common noise (i.e.,
\varepsilon=0
), the
equilibria are given as the solutions of the deterministic system

(3.7)

\dot{m}_{t}=-\bigl(\eta_{t}m_{t}+h_{t}\bigr),\quad\dot{h}_{t}=\eta_{t}h_{t},\quad t\in[0,1]\ ;\quad h_{1}=g(m_{1}),

with
m_{0}=0
,
which prompts us to perform the changes of variable:

(3.8)

\widetilde{m}_{t}=\frac{m_{t}}{2-t},\quad\widetilde{h}_{t}=(2-t)h_{t},\quad t\in[0,1].

We then have that
(m_{t},h_{t})_{0\leq t\leq 1}

solves
(
3.7
)
if and only if

\begin{split}&\dot{\widetilde{m}}_{t}=-\frac{1}{2-t}h_{t}=-\frac{1}{(2-t)^{2}}\widetilde{h}_{t},\quad\dot{\widetilde{h}}_{t}=0,\quad t\in[0,1]\ ;\quad\widetilde{h}_{1}=g\bigl(\widetilde{m}_{1}\bigr),\end{split}

from which we deduce the following simpler characterization (recalling that
m_{0}=0
)

(3.9)

\widetilde{m}_{1}=-g\bigl(\widetilde{m}_{1}\bigr)\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{(2-t)^{2}}=-\frac{g(\widetilde{m}_{1})}{2}.

There are as many equilibria as solutions to the equation

x=-g(x)/2
.
We thus have the following obvious lemma:

Lemma 3.1
.

When
\varepsilon=0
and regardless of the dimension, the solutions of the MFG associated with the cost functional
(
3.1
)
are given by the roots
\widetilde{m}_{1}
of the equation
2x+g(x)=0
and then by the changes of variable
(
3.8
). In particular,
if the Lipschitz constant of the function
g
is strictly less than 2, then the MFG has one and only one solution.

3.1.2.
Potential structure when
\varepsilon=0

Following
(
1.13
)
and
(
1.14
), we may associate
a mean field control problem with the MFG (with
\varepsilon=0
) if the function
g
derives from a potential

G
.
The cost functional is given by

{\mathcal{J}}({\bm{\alpha}})={\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}|X_{1}|^{2}+G\bigl({\mathbb{E}}(X_{1})\bigr)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}|\alpha_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr],

where, in the right-hand side,

X_{1}=\int_{0}^{1}\alpha_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

The analysis of the minimization problem
\inf_{{\bm{\alpha}}}{\mathcal{J}}({\bm{\alpha}})
is straightforward.
Indeed, by an obvious convexity argument, we observe that

{\mathcal{J}}({\bm{\alpha}})\geq{\mathcal{J}}\biggl({\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{1}\alpha_{t}\mathrm{d}t\biggr),

where, in the right-hand side, it is obviously understood that
the argument of

{\mathcal{J}}
is a constant control.
This shows that
the minimizers
are deterministic and constant in time.
Using the generic notation
\beta
for
\int_{0}^{1}\alpha_{t}\mathrm{d}t
, the problem is thus to minimize

{\mathscr{J}}(\beta)=\beta^{2}+G(\beta),\quad\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Of course, we observe that any minimizer of
{\mathscr{J}}
is also a solution of the equation

\beta=-g(\beta)/2
: we recover the fact that
any solution to the mean field control problem is an MFG solution.
Obviously, the converse may not be true. Accordingly,
the set of solutions to the mean field control problem may be strictly included in the set
of equilibria of the corresponding mean field game. This principle is illustrated by
Figure
5
below with

g
as in (
3.2
): For all the values of

\kappa\in\{2,3,\cdots,10\}
, the potential
{\mathscr{J}}
has a unique minimizer;
but, for
\kappa\in\{7,8,9,10\}
, the derivative of
{\mathscr{J}}
has several zeros, hence proving that the MFG has several solutions.

(a)

Evolution of
{\mathscr{J}}
with
\kappa
.

(b)

Evolution of
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\beta}{\mathscr{J}}
with
\kappa
.

Figure 5
.
Minimizers of the mean field control problem and equilibria of the mean field game for
\kappa\in\{2,\cdots,10\}
when
d=1
and
g(x)=\cos(\kappa x)
:
